<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the Sandier–Serfaty conjecture: the triangular lattice of covolume one minimizes planar Coulomb renormalized energy over all admissible curl-free fields with a unit uniform background. Combined with Bétermin and Sandier's asymptotic formula, this also proves the Brauchart–Hardin–Saff conjecture for the linear term of optimal ordered-pair logarithmic energy on the unit two-sphere. The proof uses a direct Voronoi-cell comparison with rigorous interval arithmetic.

- **claim-main-minimality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every cutoff family satisfying <a href="#eq:cutoffs" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cutoffs">[eq:cutoffs]</a> and every field $E\in\mathcal A_1$, $$W(E)\geq W(E_\triangle).$$ The triangular value is finite, and $\inf_{E\in\mathcal A_1}W(E)=W(E_\triangle)$.
  - Explanation: Every admissible curl-free field with unit background has renormalized energy per unit area at least that of the covolume-one triangular lattice field. The triangular value is finite and equals the infimum, so negative infinity cannot occur. This is the minimality conjectured by Sandier and Serfaty.
- **claim-torus-lower-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-torus)): For every integer $n\geq2$ and every configuration of $n$ distinct points on $T_n$, the potential in <a href="#eq:torus-energy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:torus-energy">[eq:torus-energy]</a> satisfies $$\mathcal W_n(h)\geq nW(E_\triangle).$$
  - Explanation: On every square torus of area n, any configuration of n distinct points has Green-potential renormalized energy at least n times the triangular energy. It is a lower bound only and asserts no equality on any particular square torus. It is the finite-volume input for the planar theorem.
- **claim-bhs-sphere-constant** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-bhs)): Let $S^2=\{y\in\mathbb R^3:\\|y\\|=1\}$ be the unit sphere. For each integer $n\ge2$, define $$E_{\log}(n)=\min_{(y_1,\ldots,y_n)\in(S^2)^n} \left(-\sum_{\substack{1\le i,j\le n\\i\ne j}} \log\\|y_i-y_j\\|\right),$$ where the sum is over ordered pairs, $\\|\cdot\\|$ is the Euclidean norm in $\mathbb R^3$, $\log$ is the natural logarithm, and a collision has energy $+\infty$. Then, as $n\to\infty$ through the integers, $$\label{eq:bhs-asymptotic} E_{\log}(n)=\left(\frac12-\log2\right)n^2 -\frac n2\log n+C_{\mathrm{BHS}}n+o(n),$$ where $$\label{eq:bhs-constant} C_{\mathrm{BHS}} =2\log2+\frac12\log\!\left(\frac23\right) +3\log\!\left(\frac{\sqrt\pi}{\Gamma(1/3)}\right),$$ and $\Gamma$ is Euler’s gamma function.
  - Explanation: The optimal ordered-pair logarithmic energy of n points on the unit two-sphere has a quadratic term, an n log n term, and a linear term whose coefficient is the explicit Brauchart–Hardin–Saff constant, with an error of lower order. It follows from planar minimality combined with the Bétermin–Sandier equivalence. It is an asymptotic value, not a construction of point sets.
- **lemma-local-expansion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-red-local)): Let $\Lambda\subset\mathbb R^2$ be simple and locally finite, and let $E\in\mathcal D'(\mathbb R^2;\mathbb R^2)$ be a distributional vector field satisfying $$\operatorname{div}E=2\pi\Bigl(\sum_{p\in\Lambda}\delta_p-1\Bigr), \qquad \operatorname{curl}E=0.$$ The field is smooth off $\Lambda$. Near each $p\in\Lambda$ it has the form $$\label{red:local-expansion} E(x)=\frac{x-p}{\|x-p\|^2}+F_p(x),\qquad F_p\text{ smooth}.$$ Consequently $E$ has a representative in $L^q_{\mathrm{loc}}$ for every $1\le q<2$, and $W(E,\xi)$ exists as a finite real number for every smooth compactly supported $\xi$.
  - Explanation: A distributional curl-free field with unit-charge divergence at a simple locally finite set is smooth off the charges and equals the Coulomb singularity plus a smooth field near each charge. It is therefore locally integrable to every exponent below two, and the local puncture energy exists as a finite real number.
- **lemma-plane-lower-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-red-plane-bound)): Every $E\in\mathcal A_1$ satisfies $$\label{red:plane-lower} W(E)\ge 2\pi\left(M-\frac14\log(2\pi)\right).$$ In particular $W(E)\ne-\infty$.
  - Explanation: Every admissible field has planar energy bounded below by an explicit expression in the common minimum of the current-class energy, so the energy never equals negative infinity. The proof rescales to currents and uses local energy estimates to control the boundary strip.
- **lemma-periodic-average** (theorem, [source](#stmt-red-periodic-average)): Let $F$ be a periodic admissible electric field or current, with simple charges and period cell $P$ of area $A$. For either square or ball cutoffs with fixed boundary width and uniformly bounded gradients, its energy per unit area has a limit, equal to $\mathcal E_P(F)/A$.
  - Explanation: For a periodic field with simple charges, the energy per unit area under square or ball cutoffs of fixed boundary width converges to the cell energy divided by the cell area. Boundary cells contribute only lower order.
- **prop-reduction-to-tori** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-red-reduction)): Let $C\in\mathbb R$. Suppose that for every integer $n\ge2$ and every configuration of $n$ distinct points of $T_n$, the mean-zero potential in <a href="#eq:torus-energy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:torus-energy">[eq:torus-energy]</a> satisfies $\mathcal W_n(h)\ge nC$. Then $W(E)\ge C$ for every $E\in\mathcal A_1$. In particular, if these finite-torus bounds hold with $C=W(E_\triangle)$, then $$\inf_{E\in\mathcal A_1}W(E)=W(E_\triangle).$$
  - Explanation: If a common lower bound holds for all configurations on all square tori, the same bound holds for every admissible planar field. With the triangular value as the constant, the infimum over the admissible class equals the triangular energy.
- **lemma-green-representation-existence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-existence)): Let $G_n$ be the mean-zero Green function on $T_n$, normalized by $$-\Delta G_n=2\pi(\delta_0-1/n), \qquad R_n=\lim_{x\to0}\bigl(G_n(x)+\log\|x\|\bigr).$$ For every configuration of $n$ distinct points, $$h(x)=\sum_{i=1}^nG_n(x-v_i),\qquad \mathcal W_n(h)=\pi nR_n+ \pi\sum_{i\ne j}G_n(v_i-v_j). \label{geo:pair-energy}$$ The minimum of $\mathcal W_n$ over such configurations is attained at a configuration of distinct points.
  - Explanation: The torus energy of a configuration is written through the Green function as a regular part plus a sum over ordered pairs. Because that expression is lower semicontinuous and blows up at collisions, a minimizing configuration of distinct points exists.
- **lemma-minimizer-separation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-separation)): For every $n\geq2$, a minimizing configuration in Lemma <a href="#geo:existence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geo:existence">8</a> has torus distance at least $$r_*=\frac1{\sqrt\pi}$$ between distinct points. Its full lift to $\mathbb R^2$ has the same minimum-separation bound, including between different period translates of one torus point.
  - Explanation: Points of a minimizing torus configuration stay at least a fixed core distance apart, also between lifts to the plane. The proof screens each point by a uniform disk and applies the strict minimum principle. Separation is claimed only for minimizers.
- **lemma-periodic-voronoi-geometry** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-voronoi)): Let $P\subset\mathbb R^2$ be the full $L\mathbb Z^2$-periodic lift of $n$ distinct points on $T_n$, and suppose $P$ has separation at least $r_*$. Its Voronoi polygons, modulo $L\mathbb Z^2$, have at most $6n$ face–edge incidences.* *Each such incidence determines a sector <a href="#geo:sector" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="geo:sector">[geo:sector]</a> in polar coordinates about its site, with $d\geq r_*/2$. The sectors in one torus have total area $n$ and total angle $2\pi n$. Either endpoint parameter $\alpha$ or $\beta$ is allowed to be negative.
  - Explanation: For a separated periodic point set, the Voronoi polygons modulo the period lattice have at most six face-edge incidences per point. Each incidence gives a sector with bounded-below edge distance. The sectors tile the torus, and an endpoint angle may be negative.
- **lemma-dual-gluing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-dual-gluing)): For compatible periodic Voronoi data on $\mathbb T$ with $N\geq1$ distinct sites and lower edge distance $d_0$, let $I$ be finite or countable, let $k_i\geq6$ be integers with repetitions permitted, and let $B,D_i\in C^1((0,\infty))$ satisfy <a href="#dual:radial-origin" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="dual:radial-origin">[dual:radial-origin]</a>–<a href="#dual:radial-sum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="dual:radial-sum">[dual:radial-sum]</a>. The sector functions <a href="#dual:trial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="dual:trial">[dual:trial]</a> define a $\Gamma$-periodic function $H$ that is continuous off the sites and belongs to $H^1(\Omega_\eta)$ for every admissible $\eta<d_0$. At every site, uniformly in the angular variable, $$H=-\log r+O(r^2),\qquad \nabla H=-\frac{e_r}{r}+O(r). \label{dual:singularity}$$ The gradient statement is sectorwise, and hence holds for the weak gradient almost everywhere; no agreement of derivatives across sectors is asserted. In particular the constant term in $H+\log r$ is zero.
  - Explanation: The sector formulas built from common radial functions have matching traces on edges and vertex rays, so they glue to a periodic function in the first Sobolev space off the holes. At each site the function has the logarithmic singularity with zero constant term.
- **prop-dual-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-dual-identity)): For compatible periodic Voronoi data on $\mathbb T=\mathbb R^2/\Gamma$ with $N\geq1$ distinct sites, lower edge distance $d_0>0$, and $\|\mathbb T\|=N$, let $h$ be the mean-zero potential <a href="#dual:torus-potential" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="dual:torus-potential">[dual:torus-potential]</a>. Let $I$ be finite or countable, let $k_i\geq6$ be integers with repetitions permitted, and let $B,D_i\in C^1((0,\infty))$ satisfy <a href="#dual:radial-origin" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="dual:radial-origin">[dual:radial-origin]</a>–<a href="#dual:radial-sum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="dual:radial-sum">[dual:radial-sum]</a>. Let $H$ be the function supplied by Lemma <a href="#dual:gluing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="dual:gluing">11</a>. Then $$\mathcal W_{\mathbb T}(h) =\sum_S J(S)+ \frac12\int_{\mathbb T}\|\nabla(h-H)\|^2\,\,\mathrm dx \geq\sum_S J(S). \label{dual:energy-bound}$$ The sum runs over all face–edge incidences in one torus. No incidence bound or square-torus separation hypothesis is imposed.
  - Explanation: On a compatible flat torus the renormalized energy equals the sum of the sector functionals plus a nonnegative Dirichlet term for the difference between the true potential and the glued trial potential. It holds without an incidence bound or separation assumption.
- **lemma-signed-endpoints** (theorem, [source](#stmt-dual-signed-endpoints)): Let $K<0$ and $d_0>0$. For each $d\geq d_0$, let $G(d,\cdot)$ be an even locally integrable real function on $\mathbb R$, and put $$g_d(x)=\int_0^xG(d,s)\,\,\mathrm ds.$$ Suppose that, for every $d\geq d_0$, $$2g_d(x)\geq K\quad(x\geq0),\qquad \int_0^\infty\max\{0,-G(d,s)\}\,\,\mathrm ds\leq-K. \label{dual:scalar-input}$$ Then, for every $d\geq d_0$ and every pair $\alpha,\beta$ with $\|\alpha\|,\|\beta\|<\pi/2$ and $\alpha+\beta>0$, $$g_d(d\tan\alpha)+g_d(d\tan\beta)\geq K. \label{dual:signed-bound}$$
  - Explanation: Two scalar conditions on an even kernel, a lower bound on its primitive and a bound on the integral of its negative part, give a lower bound on the sum of primitive values at both sector endpoints, including when one endpoint parameter is negative.
- **prop-sector-to-energy** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-dual-assembly)): Let $d_0>0$, let $I$ be finite or countable, let $k_i\geq6$ be integers with repetitions permitted, and let $B,D_i\in C^1((0,\infty))$ satisfy <a href="#dual:radial-origin" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="dual:radial-origin">[dual:radial-origin]</a> and <a href="#dual:radial-sum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="dual:radial-sum">[dual:radial-sum]</a> with this lower distance $d_0$. For every sector <a href="#geo:sector" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="geo:sector">[geo:sector]</a> with $d\geq d_0$, use these same radial data in <a href="#dual:trial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="dual:trial">[dual:trial]</a> and let $J(S)$ be the functional <a href="#dual:sector-functional" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="dual:sector-functional">[dual:sector-functional]</a>. Suppose that $K\leq0$ and $\lambda,\mu\in\mathbb R$ satisfy $$\begin{aligned} J(S(d,\alpha,\beta)) &\geq K+\lambda A(S(d,\alpha,\beta))\\ &\qquad+\mu\theta(S(d,\alpha,\beta)), \end{aligned} \label{dual:sector-input}$$ for every $d\geq d_0$, $\|\alpha\|,\|\beta\|<\pi/2$, and $\alpha+\beta>0$.* **Compatible tori.* Let $\mathbb T=\mathbb R^2/\Gamma$ have area $N$, where $N\geq1$ is an integer. Suppose $N$ distinct sites supply compatible periodic Voronoi data with lower edge distance $d_0$ and at most $6N$ face–edge sectors. Their mean-zero potential <a href="#dual:torus-potential" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="dual:torus-potential">[dual:torus-potential]</a> satisfies $$\label{dual:compatible-bound} \mathcal W_{\mathbb T}(h)\geq N(6K+\lambda+2\pi\mu).$$* **All square-torus configurations.* Assume in addition $d_0\leq d_*=1/(2\sqrt\pi)$. For every integer $n\geq2$ and every configuration of $n$ distinct points $v_1,\ldots,v_n$ on $T_n=\mathbb R^2/(\sqrt n\,\mathbb Z^2)$, let $h$ be the mean-zero solution of $$-\Delta h=2\pi\left(\sum_{i=1}^n\delta_{v_i}-1\right).$$ Then its puncture-first energy <a href="#eq:torus-energy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:torus-energy">[eq:torus-energy]</a>, with embedded and pairwise disjoint punctures, satisfies $$\mathcal W_n(h)\geq n(6K+\lambda+2\pi\mu). \label{dual:all-configurations}$$ More generally, if a periodic locally integrable field $E$ on $T_n$ satisfies, distributionally, $$\operatorname{div}E=2\pi\left(\sum_{i=1}^n\delta_{v_i}-1\right), \qquad \operatorname{curl}E=0,$$ then $E=-\nabla h+c$ for a constant $c\in\mathbb R^2$, and its energy with the same punctures and counterterm obeys $$\mathcal E_{T_n}(E)=\mathcal W_n(h)+\frac n2\|c\|^2 \geq n(6K+\lambda+2\pi\mu).$$ The energy identity remains valid after applying the same fixed orthogonal rotation to both fields.
  - Explanation: An affine lower bound on every sector functional yields an energy lower bound on compatible tori with at most six sectors per site. For square tori, separation of minimizers removes the geometric hypotheses and the bound holds for every configuration. A constant harmonic component only adds energy.
- **lemma-triangular-green-expansion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cal-green-expansion)): The mean-zero triangular Green function has the expansion $$\label{cal:green-series} h_\triangle(z)=-\log\|z\|+C_\triangle+\frac{\pi\|z\|^2}{2} +\sum_{m\ge1}c_m\operatorname{Re}(z^{6m}), \qquad 0<\|z\|<a,$$ where $C_\triangle$ is its regular part at the origin. The series and all of its derivatives converge locally away from $\|z\|=a$.
  - Explanation: Near the origin the mean-zero triangular Green function is the logarithm plus a constant, a quadratic term and a series in sixth-fold harmonic modes whose coefficients are lattice sums. The series converges with all derivatives inside the nearest-neighbour radius.
- **lemma-calibrated-stationarity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cal-stationarity)): For the constants <a href="#cal:constants" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cal:constants">[cal:constants]</a>, $\nabla_{d,x}g_d(x)=0$ at $(p,x_0)$.
  - Explanation: With the chosen calibration constants, the adjusted scalar primitive has vanishing gradient at the regular-hexagon sector, so that sector is a stationary point.
- **lemma-triangular-energy-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cal-energy-identity)): The calibrated constants satisfy $$\label{cal:triangular-energy} W(E_\triangle)=6K+\lambda+2\pi\mu.$$
  - Explanation: On the one-site triangular torus the trial potential has exactly the Green gradient, so the square term vanishes. The six regular sectors then give the triangular energy as the stated combination of the calibrated constants.
- **prop-scalar-bounds-to-torus** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cal-scalar-to-torus)): Assume <a href="#cal:scalar-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cal:scalar-bounds">[cal:scalar-bounds]</a> for the functions and constants just constructed. Let $\mathbb T=\mathbb R^2/\Gamma$ be a flat torus of area $N$, where $N\ge1$ is an integer, and let $v_1,\ldots,v_N$ be distinct sites. Suppose their full periodic lift gives compatible periodic Voronoi data with lower edge distance $d_0=b$ and at most $6N$ face-edge sectors. Let $h$ be the mean-zero potential <a href="#dual:torus-potential" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="dual:torus-potential">[dual:torus-potential]</a>, with energy $\mathcal W_{\mathbb T}(h)$ defined by <a href="#dual:torus-energy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="dual:torus-energy">[dual:torus-energy]</a>. Then $$\mathcal W_{\mathbb T}(h)\ge NW(E_\triangle).$$
  - Explanation: Assuming the two scalar estimates, any compatible flat torus with the stated lower edge distance and at most six sectors per site has energy at least the number of sites times the triangular energy.
- **prop-finite-assertions-soundness** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-comp-finite-soundness)): Apply the outward interval procedure specified in this section to the radial data <a href="#cal:radial-data" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cal:radial-data">[cal:radial-data]</a>, retaining modes $0$ through $38$, with the parameters in the table. If every listed assertion holds, its enclosures establish the finite-core premise <a href="#tail:finite-core-premise" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="tail:finite-core-premise">[tail:finite-core-premise]</a>, the exterior positivity <a href="#comp:positive-tail" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="comp:positive-tail">[comp:positive-tail]</a>, the primitive bound outside the interior of $\mathcal D_0$, the negative-part bound for every $d\ge b$, and the convexity bound <a href="#comp:inner-conclusion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="comp:inner-conclusion">[comp:inner-conclusion]</a>, all for the exact infinite-mode functions.
  - Explanation: If every listed finite interval assertion holds, the outward enclosures prove the finite-core premise, the exterior positivity, the primitive bound off the stationary rectangle, the negative-part bound and the convexity bound for the exact infinite-mode functions.
- **thm-scalar-inequalities** (theorem, [source](#stmt-comp-scalar)): The exact constants and functions of <a href="#cal:constants" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cal:constants">[cal:constants]</a> and <a href="#cal:primitive" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cal:primitive">[cal:primitive]</a> satisfy $$K<0,\qquad 2g_d(x)\ge K\quad(d\ge b,\ x\ge0),\qquad \int_0^\infty\max\{0,-G(d,s)\}\,\mathrm ds\le-K \quad(d\ge b).$$
  - Explanation: The exact calibration constants and functions satisfy the three scalar inequalities needed by the geometry: the stationary value is negative, the primitive is bounded below by it, and the negative part of the kernel integrates to at most its magnitude.

        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Lambda\\subset\\mathbb R^2$ be simple and locally finite, and let $E\\in\\mathcal D'(\\mathbb R^2;\\mathbb R^2)$ be a distributional vector field satisfying $$\\operatorname{div}E=2\\pi\\Bigl(\\sum_{p\\in\\Lambda}\\delta_p-1\\Bigr),\n \\qquad \\operatorname{curl}E=0.$$ The field is smooth off $\\Lambda$.         "exact_quote": "Let $C\\in\\mathbb R$.         "source_id": "PM-090.4"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geo-existence",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G_n$ be the mean-zero Green function on $T_n$, normalized by $$-\\Delta G_n=2\\pi(\\delta_0-1/n),\n \\qquad\n R_n=\\lim_{x\\to0}\\bigl(G_n(x)+\\log|x|\\bigr).$$ For every configuration of $n$ distinct points, $$h(x)=\\sum_{i=1}^nG_n(x-v_i),\\qquad\n \\mathcal W_n(h)=\\pi nR_n+\n                    \\pi\\sum_{i\\ne j}G_n(v_i-v_j).\n \\label{geo:pair-energy}$$ The minimum of $\\mathcal W_n$ over such configurations is attained at a configuration of distinct points.",
        "source_id": "PM-090.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-geo-existence",
          "exact_quote": "Let $G_n$ be the mean-zero Green function on $T_n$, normalized by $$-\\Delta G_n=2\\pi(\\delta_0-1/n),\n \\qquad\n R_n=\\lim_{x\\to0}\\bigl(G_n(x)+\\log|x|\\bigr).$$ For every configuration of $n$ distinct points, $$h(x)=\\sum_{i=1}^nG_n(x-v_i),\\qquad\n \\mathcal W_n(h)=\\pi nR_n+\n                    \\pi\\sum_{i\\ne j}G_n(v_i-v_j).\n \\label{geo:pair-energy}$$ The minimum of $\\mathcal W_n$ over such configurations is attained at a configuration of distinct points.",
          "source_id": "PM-090.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $G_n$ be the mean-zero Green function on $T_n$, normalized by $$-\\Delta G_n=2\\pi(\\delta_0-1/n),\n \\qquad\n R_n=\\lim_{x\\to0}\\bigl(G_n(x)+\\log|x|\\bigr).$$ For every configuration of $n$ distinct points, $$h(x)=\\sum_{i=1}^nG_n(x-v_i),\\qquad\n \\mathcal W_n(h)=\\pi nR_n+\n                    \\pi\\sum_{i\\ne j}G_n(v_i-v_j).\n \\label{geo:pair-energy}$$ The minimum of $\\mathcal W_n$ over such configurations is attained at a configuration of distinct points.",
      "explanation": "The torus energy of a configuration is written through the Green function as a regular part plus a sum over ordered pairs. Because that expression is lower semicontinuous and blows up at collisions, a minimizing configuration of distinct points exists.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geo-voronoi",
        "exact_quote": "Let $P\\subset\\mathbb R^2$ be the full $L\\mathbb Z^2$-periodic lift of $n$ distinct points on $T_n$, and suppose $P$ has separation at least $r_*$. Its Voronoi polygons, modulo $L\\mathbb Z^2$, have at most $6n$ face–edge incidences.*\n\n*Each such incidence determines a sector <a href=\"#geo:sector\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"geo:sector\">[geo:sector]</a> in polar coordinates about its site, with $d\\geq r_*/2$.         "source_id": "PM-090.4"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-dual-gluing",
        "exact_quote": "For compatible periodic Voronoi data on $\\mathbb T$ with $N\\geq1$ distinct sites and lower edge distance $d_0$, let $I$ be finite or countable, let $k_i\\geq6$ be integers with repetitions permitted, and let $B,D_i\\in C^1((0,\\infty))$ satisfy <a href=\"#dual:radial-origin\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"dual:radial-origin\">[dual:radial-origin]</a>–<a href=\"#dual:radial-sum\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"dual:radial-sum\">[dual:radial-sum]</a>. The sector functions <a href=\"#dual:trial\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"dual:trial\">[dual:trial]</a> define a $\\Gamma$-periodic function $H$ that is continuous off the sites and belongs to $H^1(\\Omega_\\eta)$ for every admissible $\\eta<d_0$. At every site, uniformly in the angular variable, $$H=-\\log r+O(r^2),\\qquad\n \\nabla H=-\\frac{e_r}{r}+O(r).\n \\label{dual:singularity}$$ The gradient statement is sectorwise, and hence holds for the weak gradient almost everywhere; no agreement of derivatives across sectors is asserted. In particular the constant term in $H+\\log r$ is zero.",
        "source_id": "PM-090.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-dual-gluing",
          "exact_quote": "For compatible periodic Voronoi data on $\\mathbb T$ with $N\\geq1$ distinct sites and lower edge distance $d_0$, let $I$ be finite or countable, let $k_i\\geq6$ be integers with repetitions permitted, and let $B,D_i\\in C^1((0,\\infty))$ satisfy <a href=\"#dual:radial-origin\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"dual:radial-origin\">[dual:radial-origin]</a>–<a href=\"#dual:radial-sum\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"dual:radial-sum\">[dual:radial-sum]</a>. The sector functions <a href=\"#dual:trial\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"dual:trial\">[dual:trial]</a> define a $\\Gamma$-periodic function $H$ that is continuous off the sites and belongs to $H^1(\\Omega_\\eta)$ for every admissible $\\eta<d_0$. At every site, uniformly in the angular variable, $$H=-\\log r+O(r^2),\\qquad\n \\nabla H=-\\frac{e_r}{r}+O(r).\n \\label{dual:singularity}$$ The gradient statement is sectorwise, and hence holds for the weak gradient almost everywhere; no agreement of derivatives across sectors is asserted. In particular the constant term in $H+\\log r$ is zero.",
          "source_id": "PM-090.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For compatible periodic Voronoi data on $\\mathbb T$ with $N\\geq1$ distinct sites and lower edge distance $d_0$, let $I$ be finite or countable, let $k_i\\geq6$ be integers with repetitions permitted, and let $B,D_i\\in C^1((0,\\infty))$ satisfy <a href=\"#dual:radial-origin\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"dual:radial-origin\">[dual:radial-origin]</a>–<a href=\"#dual:radial-sum\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"dual:radial-sum\">[dual:radial-sum]</a>. At every site, uniformly in the angular variable, $$H=-\\log r+O(r^2),\\qquad\n \\nabla H=-\\frac{e_r}{r}+O(r).\n \\label{dual:singularity}$$ The gradient statement is sectorwise, and hence holds for the weak gradient almost everywhere; no agreement of derivatives across sectors is asserted. In particular the constant term in $H+\\log r$ is zero.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-dual-identity",
        "exact_quote": "For compatible periodic Voronoi data on $\\mathbb T=\\mathbb R^2/\\Gamma$ with $N\\geq1$ distinct sites, lower edge distance $d_0>0$, and $|\\mathbb T|=N$, let $h$ be the mean-zero potential <a href=\"#dual:torus-potential\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"dual:torus-potential\">[dual:torus-potential]</a>. Let $I$ be finite or countable, let $k_i\\geq6$ be integers with repetitions permitted, and let $B,D_i\\in C^1((0,\\infty))$ satisfy <a href=\"#dual:radial-origin\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"dual:radial-origin\">[dual:radial-origin]</a>–<a href=\"#dual:radial-sum\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"dual:radial-sum\">[dual:radial-sum]</a>. Let $H$ be the function supplied by Lemma <a href=\"#dual:gluing\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"dual:gluing\">11</a>. Then $$\\mathcal W_{\\mathbb T}(h)\n   =\\sum_S J(S)+\n              \\frac12\\int_{\\mathbb T}|\\nabla(h-H)|^2\\,\\,\\mathrm dx\n   \\geq\\sum_S J(S).\n \\label{dual:energy-bound}$$ The sum runs over all face–edge incidences in one torus.       "exact_statement": "For compatible periodic Voronoi data on $\\mathbb T=\\mathbb R^2/\\Gamma$ with $N\\geq1$ distinct sites, lower edge distance $d_0>0$, and $|\\mathbb T|=N$, let $h$ be the mean-zero potential <a href=\"#dual:torus-potential\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"dual:torus-potential\">[dual:torus-potential]</a>. Let $I$ be finite or countable, let $k_i\\geq6$ be integers with repetitions permitted, and let $B,D_i\\in C^1((0,\\infty))$ satisfy <a href=\"#dual:radial-origin\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"dual:radial-origin\">[dual:radial-origin]</a>–<a href=\"#dual:radial-sum\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"dual:radial-sum\">[dual:radial-sum]</a>. Let $H$ be the function supplied by Lemma <a href=\"#dual:gluing\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"dual:gluing\">11</a>. Then $$\\mathcal W_{\\mathbb T}(h)\n   =\\sum_S J(S)+\n              \\frac12\\int_{\\mathbb T}|\\nabla(h-H)|^2\\,\\,\\mathrm dx\n   \\geq\\sum_S J(S).\n \\label{dual:energy-bound}$$ The sum runs over all face–edge incidences in one torus. No incidence bound or square-torus separation hypothesis is imposed.",
      "explanation": "On a compatible flat torus the renormalized energy equals the sum of the sector functionals plus a nonnegative Dirichlet term for the difference between the true potential and the glued trial potential. It holds without an incidence bound or separation assumption.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-dual-signed-endpoints",
        "exact_quote": "Let $K<0$ and $d_0>0$. For each $d\\geq d_0$, let $G(d,\\cdot)$ be an even locally integrable real function on $\\mathbb R$, and put $$g_d(x)=\\int_0^xG(d,s)\\,\\,\\mathrm ds.$$ Suppose that, for every $d\\geq d_0$, $$2g_d(x)\\geq K\\quad(x\\geq0),\\qquad\n \\int_0^\\infty\\max\\{0,-G(d,s)\\}\\,\\,\\mathrm ds\\leq-K.\n \\label{dual:scalar-input}$$ Then, for every $d\\geq d_0$ and every pair $\\alpha,\\beta$ with $|\\alpha|,|\\beta|<\\pi/2$ and $\\alpha+\\beta>0$, $$g_d(d\\tan\\alpha)+g_d(d\\tan\\beta)\\geq K.\n \\label{dual:signed-bound}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-090.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-dual-signed-endpoints",
          "exact_quote": "Let $K<0$ and $d_0>0$.           "source_id": "PM-090.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $K<0$ and $d_0>0$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-dual-assembly",
        "exact_quote": "Let $d_0>0$, let $I$ be finite or countable, let $k_i\\geq6$ be integers with repetitions permitted, and let $B,D_i\\in C^1((0,\\infty))$ satisfy <a href=\"#dual:radial-origin\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"dual:radial-origin\">[dual:radial-origin]</a> and <a href=\"#dual:radial-sum\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"dual:radial-sum\">[dual:radial-sum]</a> with this lower distance $d_0$. For every sector <a href=\"#geo:sector\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"geo:sector\">[geo:sector]</a> with $d\\geq d_0$, use these same radial data in <a href=\"#dual:trial\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"dual:trial\">[dual:trial]</a> and let $J(S)$ be the functional <a href=\"#dual:sector-functional\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"dual:sector-functional\">[dual:sector-functional]</a>. Suppose that $K\\leq0$ and $\\lambda,\\mu\\in\\mathbb R$ satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n J(S(d,\\alpha,\\beta))\n &\\geq K+\\lambda A(S(d,\\alpha,\\beta))\\\\\n &\\qquad+\\mu\\theta(S(d,\\alpha,\\beta)),\n \\end{aligned}\n \\label{dual:sector-input}$$ for every $d\\geq d_0$, $|\\alpha|,|\\beta|<\\pi/2$, and $\\alpha+\\beta>0$.*\n\n\n**Compatible tori.* Let $\\mathbb T=\\mathbb R^2/\\Gamma$ have area $N$, where $N\\geq1$ is an integer. Suppose $N$ distinct sites supply compatible periodic Voronoi data with lower edge distance $d_0$ and at most $6N$ face–edge sectors. Their mean-zero potential <a href=\"#dual:torus-potential\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"dual:torus-potential\">[dual:torus-potential]</a> satisfies $$\\label{dual:compatible-bound}\n \\mathcal W_{\\mathbb T}(h)\\geq N(6K+\\lambda+2\\pi\\mu).$$*\n\n\n**All square-torus configurations.* Assume in addition $d_0\\leq d_*=1/(2\\sqrt\\pi)$. For every integer $n\\geq2$ and every configuration of $n$ distinct points $v_1,\\ldots,v_n$ on $T_n=\\mathbb R^2/(\\sqrt n\\,\\mathbb Z^2)$, let $h$ be the mean-zero solution of $$-\\Delta h=2\\pi\\left(\\sum_{i=1}^n\\delta_{v_i}-1\\right).$$ Then its puncture-first energy <a href=\"#eq:torus-energy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:torus-energy\">[eq:torus-energy]</a>, with embedded and pairwise disjoint punctures, satisfies $$\\mathcal W_n(h)\\geq n(6K+\\lambda+2\\pi\\mu).\n \\label{dual:all-configurations}$$ More generally, if a periodic locally integrable field $E$ on $T_n$ satisfies, distributionally, $$\\operatorname{div}E=2\\pi\\left(\\sum_{i=1}^n\\delta_{v_i}-1\\right),\n \\qquad \\operatorname{curl}E=0,$$ then $E=-\\nabla h+c$ for a constant $c\\in\\mathbb R^2$, and its energy with the same punctures and counterterm obeys $$\\mathcal E_{T_n}(E)=\\mathcal W_n(h)+\\frac n2|c|^2\n                    \\geq n(6K+\\lambda+2\\pi\\mu).$$ The energy identity remains valid after applying the same fixed orthogonal rotation to both fields.",
        "source_id": "PM-090.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-dual-assembly",

Suppose that $K\\leq0$ and $\\lambda,\\mu\\in\\mathbb R$ satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n J(S(d,\\alpha,\\beta))\n &\\geq K+\\lambda A(S(d,\\alpha,\\beta))\\\\\n &\\qquad+\\mu\\theta(S(d,\\alpha,\\beta)),\n \\end{aligned}\n \\label{dual:sector-input}$$ for every $d\\geq d_0$, $|\\alpha|,|\\beta|<\\pi/2$, and $\\alpha+\\beta>0$.*\n\n\n**Compatible tori.* Let $\\mathbb T=\\mathbb R^2/\\Gamma$ have area $N$, where $N\\geq1$ is an integer. For every integer $n\\geq2$ and every configuration of $n$ distinct points $v_1,\\ldots,v_n$ on $T_n=\\mathbb R^2/(\\sqrt n\\,\\mathbb Z^2)$, let $h$ be the mean-zero solution of $$-\\Delta h=2\\pi\\left(\\sum_{i=1}^n\\delta_{v_i}-1\\right).$$ Then its puncture-first energy <a href=\"#eq:torus-energy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:torus-energy\">[eq:torus-energy]</a>, with embedded and pairwise disjoint punctures, satisfies $$\\mathcal W_n(h)\\geq n(6K+\\lambda+2\\pi\\mu).\n \\label{dual:all-configurations}$$ More generally, if a periodic locally integrable field $E$ on $T_n$ satisfies, distributionally, $$\\operatorname{div}E=2\\pi\\left(\\sum_{i=1}^n\\delta_{v_i}-1\\right),\n \\qquad \\operatorname{curl}E=0,$$ then $E=-\\nabla h+c$ for a constant $c\\in\\mathbb R^2$, and its energy with the same punctures and counterterm obeys $$\\mathcal E_{T_n}(E)=\\mathcal W_n(h)+\\frac n2|c|^2\n                    \\geq n(6K+\\lambda+2\\pi\\mu).$$ The energy identity remains valid after applying the same fixed orthogonal rotation to both fields.",
      "explanation": "An affine lower bound on every sector functional yields an energy lower bound on compatible tori with at most six sectors per site.         "exact_quote": "The mean-zero triangular Green function has the expansion $$\\label{cal:green-series}\n h_\\triangle(z)=-\\log|z|+C_\\triangle+\\frac{\\pi|z|^2}{2}\n                 +\\sum_{m\\ge1}c_m\\operatorname{Re}(z^{6m}),\n \\qquad 0<|z|<a,$$ where $C_\\triangle$ is its regular part at the origin.         "exact_quote": "Assume <a href=\"#cal:scalar-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cal:scalar-bounds\">[cal:scalar-bounds]</a> for the functions and constants just constructed. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Admissible class A_1** ([source](#pm-h-3)): The admissible class $\mathcal A_1$ consists of locally integrable vector fields $E$ such that $$\label{eq:admissible} \operatorname{div}E=2\pi(\nu_\Lambda-1),\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{curl}}E=0,\qquad \sup_{R>1}\frac{\nu_\Lambda(B_R)}{\|B_R\|}<\infty,$$ where the equations hold in distributions and $B_R$ is the disk of radius $R$ centered at the origin.
- **Charge measure** ([source](#eq:cutoffs)): Let $\Lambda\subset\mathbb R^2$ be locally finite and simple, so every point has multiplicity one, and put $\nu_\Lambda=\sum_{p\in\Lambda}\delta_p$.
- **Triangular reference field** ([source](#eq:triangular-lattice)): Let $h_\triangle$ be the mean-zero periodic distributional solution of $-\Delta h_\triangle=2\pi(\delta_0-1)$ on $\mathbb R^2/\Lambda_\triangle$, and set $E_\triangle=-\nabla h_\triangle$.
- **Square torus** ([source](#eq:torus-energy)): For an integer $n\geq2$, write $T_n=\mathbb R^2/(\sqrt n\,\mathbb Z^2)$.
- **Admissible puncture radius** ([source](#pm-h-16)): Call a radius $\eta>0$ admissible if it is smaller than the injectivity radius of $\mathbb T$ and, when $N\geq2$, smaller than half the minimum torus distance between distinct sites.
- **Compatible periodic Voronoi data** ([source](#pm-h-16)): We call these *compatible periodic Voronoi data with lower edge distance $d_0$*.
- **Triangular cell geometry constants** ([source](#cal:geometry)): Thus $p$ and $R_0$ are the inradius and circumradius of the covolume-one triangular Voronoi hexagon, and $x_0=p\tan(\pi/6)$.
- **Auxiliary mode index zero** ([source](#cal:coefficients)): We also introduce an auxiliary index $0$, with $k_0=6$.
- **Truncation convention** ([source](#tail:section)): Tildes will always mean that the modes $m\geq39$ have been omitted; the index-zero term is retained.
- **Integer interval representation** ([source](#comp:arithmetic)): A scalar interval is represented by integers $u\le v$, meaning $[u/q,v/q]$.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-separation-radius | minimum separation of a minimizing torus configuration | = | $\frac1{\sqrt\pi}$ | minimizing configurations only |
| q-incidence-bound | face-edge incidences of Voronoi polygons modulo the period lattice | ≤ | $6n$ | no generic-position assumption |
| q-edge-distance-lower | edge distance of each sector | ≥ | $r_*/2$ | each face-edge incidence |
| q-main-lower-bound | planar renormalized energy of an admissible field | ≥ | $W(E_\triangle)$ | all fields, including infinite energy |
| q-torus-lower-bound | torus energy of n distinct points | ≥ | $nW(E_\triangle)$ | lower bound only, no equality asserted |
| q-plane-lower-bound | planar energy in terms of the common current-class minimum | ≥ | $2\pi\left(M-\frac14\log(2\pi)\right)$ | all admissible fields |
| q-triangular-energy-calibration | triangular energy via calibrated constants | = | $6K+\lambda+2\pi\mu$ | exact constants, not rounded |
| q-scalar-threshold-b | lower edge distance threshold b | = | $28209/100000$ | domain d at least b |
| q-lambda-range | calibration multiplier lambda | < | $-6.729$ | accepted only when interval endpoints prove it |
| q-stationary-value-sign | stationary value K | < | $0$ | exact constant |
| q-truncation-error | absolute truncation error of the radial functions and derivatives | < | $10^{-11}$ | six quantities: two functions and their first two derivatives |
| q-omitted-weight-sum | weighted sum of omitted mode bounds | < | $2\cdot10^{-21}$ | mode indices from the first omitted one onward |
| q-interval-denominator | fixed denominator of the interval arithmetic | = | $2^{144}$ | all enclosures of the finite verification |
| q-lambda-enclosure | recorded projection of lambda | ~ | $[-6.7300312,-6.7300275]$ | outward-rounded projection of exact endpoints |
| q-K-enclosure | recorded projection of K | ~ | $[-.0034823,-.0034809]$ | outward-rounded projection of exact endpoints |
| q-derivative-bound-L1 | global bound on the first distance derivative of the kernel | ≤ | $7.4636170$ | projection of a computed hull, not a separate threshold assertion |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-local-expansion** [claim](#stmt-red-local) / [proof](#proof-stmt-red-local): Subtract the Coulomb singularity and apply Weyl's lemma, then integrate the puncture terms. -> smooth off the charges, Coulomb form near each charge, finite puncture limit
- **step-plane-lower-bound** [claim](#stmt-red-plane-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-red-plane-bound): Rescale to currents, bound the boundary strip with local estimates and mass displacement, compare cutoffs. -> explicit lower bound on planar energy
- **step-periodic-average** [claim](#stmt-red-periodic-average) / [proof](#proof-stmt-red-periodic-average): Split off the singular part on each charge and integrate over complete period cells. -> energy per unit area equals cell energy over cell area
- **step-reduction** [claim](#stmt-red-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-red-reduction): Approximate by square-periodic currents, rescale to a square torus, use the harmonic-component identity and the plane lower bound. -> torus bound transfers to every admissible field
- **step-existence** [claim](#stmt-geo-existence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-existence): Green representation, then lower semicontinuity on a compact space. -> a minimizing configuration of distinct points
- **step-separation** [claim](#stmt-geo-separation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-separation): Screen the other points by a uniform disk and apply the strict minimum principle. -> core separation for minimizers
- **step-voronoi-count** [claim](#stmt-geo-voronoi) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-voronoi): Euler's identity and a degree count on the cell decomposition of the torus. -> at most six incidences per point; sector decomposition
- **step-gluing** [claim](#stmt-dual-gluing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-dual-gluing): Match traces on edges and vertex rays and apply Sobolev gluing. -> periodic trial potential with the logarithmic singularity
- **step-dual-identity** [claim](#stmt-dual-identity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-dual-identity): Green's identity on the punctured torus, expansion of the square and partition into sectors. -> exact identity with a nonnegative square
- **step-signed-endpoints** [claim](#stmt-dual-signed-endpoints) / [proof](#proof-stmt-dual-signed-endpoints): Oddness of the primitive and the negative-part integral bound handle a negative endpoint. -> lower bound for the sum of endpoint primitives
- **step-sector-to-energy** [claim](#stmt-dual-assembly) / [proof](#proof-stmt-dual-assembly): Sum the affine sector bounds with the incidence count, then reduce square tori to minimizers. -> energy lower bound for all square-torus configurations
- **step-green-expansion** [claim](#stmt-cal-green-expansion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cal-green-expansion): Identify the second complex derivative with the Weierstrass function and expand using rotation symmetry. -> expansion in sixth-fold modes
- **step-stationarity** [claim](#stmt-cal-stationarity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cal-stationarity): Differentiate along a fixed angle, integrate by parts, and use the definitions of the constants. -> vanishing gradient at the regular sector
- **step-triangular-energy** [claim](#stmt-cal-energy-identity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cal-energy-identity): Apply the dual identity on the one-site triangular torus where the trial gradient equals the Green gradient. -> triangular energy in terms of calibrated constants
- **step-scalar-to-torus** [claim](#stmt-cal-scalar-to-torus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cal-scalar-to-torus): Combine the signed-endpoint lemma and primitive identity with the compatible-torus bound. -> torus lower bound for compatible data
- **step-finite-soundness** [claim](#stmt-comp-finite-soundness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-comp-finite-soundness): Outward interval enclosures plus analytic tail bounds, induction over radial steps, derivative and quadrature errors, and Hessian positivity. -> finite assertions imply bounds for the exact functions
- **step-scalar-inequalities** [claim](#stmt-comp-scalar) / [proof](#proof-stmt-comp-scalar): Combine the verified finite assertions with exterior positivity and monotonicity of the primitive. -> the three scalar inequalities
- **step-torus-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-torus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-comp-scalar-2): Apply the square-torus conclusion with the certified scalar bounds and the exact triangular calibration. -> torus lower bound for every configuration
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-comp-scalar-3): Apply the reduction proposition to the torus bound with the triangular value as constant. -> planar minimality
- **step-sphere-corollary** [claim](#stmt-cor-bhs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-comp-scalar-4): Match normalizations with the Bétermin–Sandier framework and use their equivalence clause. -> explicit linear coefficient on the sphere

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-real-recurrence**: For a nonzero lattice coordinate pair, the real parts of the sixth-power-multiple powers of the associated complex number satisfy an integer three-term recurrence starting from 1 and an explicit degree-six polynomial.
- **inv-binomial-numerator**: The recurrence value coincides with the binomial-sum expression for the same numerator.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-sphere-point-construction**: Use the sphere corollary to generate near-optimal point sets or solve the seventh problem of Smale. -> The corollary gives only an asymptotic energy value, with no coordinates or algorithm.
- **anti-square-torus-equality**: Assume the triangular energy density is attained on a given square torus. -> The torus theorem is only a lower bound.
- **anti-shell-grouping**: Replace the shell sum of products by one recurrence value times the shell sum. -> Different points of one norm can have different first-mode values, so the shortcut gives wrong sums.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The appendix gives a finite exact integer recurrence for the real parts of powers of lattice-point numerators, which is checked against the binomial expression. The main theorems are not finite procedures and are not covered.

```python
# INPUTS: one line with three integers n q m (lattice coordinates and mode index)
# PRECONDITIONS: (n,q) is not (0,0); m >= 0
# OUTPUTS: the integer real part of zeta^(6m) where zeta = (2n+q) + i*sqrt(3)*q
# TIME: O(m) big-integer multiplications
# SPACE: O(1) big integers
# ARITHMETIC: exact Python integers
# FAILURE: malformed input raises an exception and a nonzero exit
import sys
n, q, m = map(int, sys.stdin.read().split())
A = 2*n + q
N = n*n+n*q+q*q
r1 = A**6 - 45*A**4*q**2 + 135*A**2*q**4 - 27*q**6
if m == 0:
    print(1)
else:
    a, b = 1, r1
    for _ in range(m-1):
        a, b = b, 2*r1*b - (4*N)**6*a
    print(b)
```

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves an energy lower bound and certifies scalar inequalities; it ships no reusable library interface or procedure for programmers to call. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No dynamics, sampling scheme or time stepping is given; the result is a static ground-state energy bound for planar charge fields. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite interval verification is a one-off certificate for this proof, not a general-purpose tool; the paper states no tool interface. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The theorems are lower bounds and existence statements; the only procedure is a fixed verification of specific inequalities, not an algorithm solving a problem family. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No data structure is introduced; the Voronoi cells and sectors are analytic objects used inside a proof. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper concerns hardware; the fixed-denominator integer interval arithmetic is a proof device. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No hardness assumption, protocol or security notion appears; the work is about logarithmic Coulomb energy. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No learning problem, loss or training procedure is involved; the energy is a fixed electrostatic functional. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | weak | inspirational | inferred | The main theorem identifies the exact minimum of a nonconvex planar energy over a large admissible class, a useful reference value for energy-minimisation benchmarks, though no solver is supplied. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No communication, consensus or fault model appears anywhere in the paper. |
| other (mathematical physics benchmarking) | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The result gives an exact ground-state energy density for two-dimensional logarithmic Coulomb systems with uniform background, and the sphere asymptotic constant, usable as validation targets for numerical studies. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-torus-lower-bound-only** (formal_scope): The square-torus theorem is a lower bound for every configuration of distinct points. It does not say the triangular energy is attained on any particular square torus. Blocked use: ['Assuming a simulation on a square periodic box reaches the triangular energy density', 'Treating the torus bound as a tight value for a given n']
- **limit-sphere-asymptotic-only** (nonconstructive): The sphere corollary fixes an asymptotic value of the optimal ordered-pair logarithmic energy. It supplies neither point sets nor an algorithm. Blocked use: ['Generating near-optimal spherical point configurations from the corollary', "Claiming progress on an algorithm for Smale's seventh problem", 'Reading the o(n) error as a finite-n guarantee']
- **limit-no-classification-of-minimizers** (formal_scope): The result concerns energy per unit area. Compact changes to a configuration leave that value unchanged, so minimality does not give uniqueness or defect exclusion. Blocked use: ['Asserting every minimizing configuration is the triangular lattice', 'Using the theorem to rule out localized defects']
- **limit-separation-minimizers-only** (hypothesis): The core separation distance is proved only for minimizing configurations on the square torus. Arbitrary competitors are handled through minimality, not through a separation property of their own. Blocked use: ['Applying the separation radius to an arbitrary non-minimizing configuration', 'Using the Voronoi sector count for a configuration that is not separated']
- **limit-compatible-voronoi-data** (hypothesis): The gluing and dual identity are stated for compatible periodic Voronoi data, which is conditional geometric input with a lower edge distance. It is not a claim about arbitrary competitors. Blocked use: ['Applying the dual identity to point sets whose Voronoi sectors have no positive lower edge distance', 'Treating the compatibility condition as automatically true for any torus configuration']
- **limit-existence-nonconstructive** (nonconstructive): A minimizing torus configuration is obtained from compactness and lower semicontinuity. No procedure for locating it is given. Blocked use: ['Presenting the existence lemma as an optimization algorithm', 'Assuming the minimizer has an explicit description']
- **limit-no-uniform-tail-error** (approximation): The enclosures of the infinite-mode functions are propagated through an exact exterior formula. No radius-independent absolute error is asserted for arbitrarily large radius. Blocked use: ['Quoting the truncation error threshold at unbounded radius', 'Reusing the table enclosures outside the stated radial interval']
- **limit-two-dimensional-logarithmic** (formal_scope): The argument rests on the two-dimensional logarithmic Coulomb interaction with a uniform unit background and curl-free fields. Blocked use: ['Applying the minimality to three-dimensional Coulomb or other power-law interactions', 'Applying it to non-uniform backgrounds or fields with nonzero curl']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the Sandier–Serfaty conjecture: the triangular lattice of covolume one minimizes planar Coulomb renormalized energy over all admissible curl-free fields with a unit uniform background. Combined with Bétermin and Sandier's asymptotic formula, this also proves the Brauchart–Hardin–Saff conjecture for the linear term of optimal ordered-pair logarithmic energy on the unit two-sphere. The proof uses a direct Voronoi-cell comparison with rigorous interval arithmetic.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

| anchor | kind | label | number | first sentence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [stmt-thm-main](#stmt-thm-main) | theorem | `thm:main` | 1 | For every cutoff family satisfying [eq:cutoffs] and every field $E\in\mathcal A_1$, $$W(E)\geq W(E_\triangle).$$ The triangular value is finite, and $\inf_{E\in\mathcal A_1}W(E)=W(E_\triangle)$.* |
| [stmt-thm-torus](#stmt-thm-torus) | theorem | `thm:torus` | 2 | For every integer $n\geq2$ and every configuration of $n$ distinct points on $T_n$, the potential in [eq:torus-energy] satisfies $$\mathcal W_n(h)\geq nW(E_\triangle).$$* |
| [stmt-cor-bhs](#stmt-cor-bhs) | corollary | `cor:bhs` | 3 | Let $S^2=\{y\in\mathbb R^3:\&#124;y\&#124;=1\}$ be the unit sphere. |
